## Economy

Main article: [Economy of Egypt](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Economy_of_Egypt)



Change in per capita GDP of Egypt, 1820–2018. Figures are inflation-adjusted to 2011 International dollars.

The economy of Egypt is the second-largest in [Africa](5334607.md) and ranks [44th worldwide](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_countries_by_GDP_\(nominal\)) as of 2025.[^cite_note-IMFAPR2025-187] It is a major [emerging market](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Emerging_market) economy, marked by its membership in organisations like the [African Union](10634933.md), [Arab League](52625.md), [BRICS](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/BRICS), and its participation in the [African Continental Free Trade Area](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/African_Continental_Free_Trade_Area) (AfCFTA).[^cite_note-intro1-188] The Egyptian economy has evolved significantly since the early 2000s, transitioning towards a market-oriented system through fiscal and monetary reforms, privatisation efforts, and foreign investment incentives. These measures have contributed to macroeconomic stability and improved unemployment and poverty rates.[^cite_note-\:2-189][^cite_note-190]&#x20;

Despite waves of privatisation and fiscal reforms, the government remains one of the country's largest employers, and state contracts continue to stimulate activity in other sectors of the still partly [socialised economy](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Socialist_economics).[^cite_note-britannica-economy-191] Construction, particularly large-scale [public works](323158.md), has remained a major source of employment and public expenditure.[^cite_note-britannica-economy-191] However, as part of an \$8 billion [International Monetary Fund](15251.md) programme launched in 2024, Egypt has committed to rationalising such spending.[^cite_note-192] As a result, private investments rose to 47.5% of total executed investments in FY 2024/25, up from 39.6%, while public investment fell to 43.3%. This marks the highest private share in five years and represents a clear shift in Egypt's investment landscape.[^cite_note-193]&#x20;



The central business district in Egypt's [new capital](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/New_Administrative_Capital)

Egypt is among the world's top five recipients of [remittances](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Remittances),[^cite_note-remittances-dne-194] which have more than doubled over the past decade, rising from \$17.1 billion in FY 2015/2016 to a record \$36.5 billion in FY 2024/2025, according to data from the [Central Bank of Egypt](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Central_Bank_of_Egypt).[^cite_note-195] These inflows are crucial for Egypt's foreign reserves and household incomes.[^cite_note-egypt.iom.int-196]&#x20;

International trade is a key component of Egypt's economy, accounting for 40% of GDP according to the [World Bank](36752.md). The country has pursued broader economic integration through free trade agreements, including the EU-Egypt Association Agreement and [AfCFTA](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/African_Continental_Free_Trade_Area).[^cite_note-trade-lloyd-197] Egypt's exports have seen significant growth in the past years,[^cite_note-OEC2023-198] and the government aims to raise them to \$145 billion by 2030.[^cite_note-199]&#x20;

Since 1979, Egypt has received substantial [United States foreign aid](22557286.md), averaging \$1.5 billion annually, with approximately \$200 million allocated to economic assistance and the remainder directed towards military aid for procuring weapon systems from American suppliers.&#x20;

### Manufacturing

See also: [Automotive industry in Egypt](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Automotive_industry_in_Egypt)



The fully electric [MCV](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Manufacturing_Commercial_Vehicles) C127 EV, made in Egypt for the German market

Egypt has the largest manufacturing sector in Africa, accounting for approximately 22% of the continent's total manufacturing value.[^cite_note-200] Under [Egypt Vision 2030](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Egypt_Vision_2030) and its related development plans, the country has relied on [industrial parks](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Industrial_parks), [free zones](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Free-trade_zone) and [special economic zones](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Special_economic_zones) to boost industrialisation and manufacturing.[^cite_note-201]&#x20;

The industrial base is diverse, spanning [chemicals](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Chemical_products), [electronics](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Electronics), [steel](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Steel), [automotive manufacturing](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Automotive_manufacturing), [pharmaceuticals](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Pharmaceuticals), and [textiles](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Textiles). The chemical industry, one of the largest, includes [plastics](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Plastics), [rubber](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Rubber), [detergents](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Detergents), [fertilisers](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Fertilizers), and [glass](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Glass), with [petrochemicals](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Petrochemicals) alone contributing about 12% of industrial output, while total sector exports are projected to reach \$9 billion in 2024.[^cite_note-202][^cite_note-203]&#x20;

Light manufacturing is one of Egypt's main industries, and several government-led programmes aim to grow it further.[^cite_note-204] The *Egypt Makes Electronics* initiative has attracted factories from international firms, including [Vivo](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Vivo_\(technology_company\)), [Infinix](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Infinix), [Oppo](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Oppo), [Xiaomi](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Xiaomi), [Nokia](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Nokia), [Electrolux](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Electrolux), [Samsung](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Samsung), [Hisense](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hisense), [Beko](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Beko), and [Haier](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Haier). These plants have made Egypt a regional centre for electronics and home appliance production.[^cite_note-205][^cite_note-electronics-206][^cite_note-appliances-207] The [textile industry](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Textile_industry) is also a cornerstone of Egypt's economy, contributing around 12% of export earnings and employing 2.5 million people. It spans the entire production chain from cotton cultivation to finished garments and is globally recognised for its premium [Giza cotton](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Gossypium_barbadense#Egyptian).[^cite_note-208][^cite_note-209]&#x20;

In addition to light manufacturing, the economy also includes a heavy manufacturing sector. Egypt is Africa's leading [steel](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Steel) producer and ranks 20th globally, with 9.8 million tons in 2022.[^cite_note-steel-210] Egyptian steel company [EZDK](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/EZDK) is the largest in the region, ranked 77th globally in 2020.[^cite_note-211] The country's [automotive industry](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Automotive_industry_in_Egypt), led by [Nasr](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/El_Nasr_Automotive_Manufacturing_Company), [Ghabbour](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ghabbour_Group), and [MCV](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Manufacturing_Commercial_Vehicles), is expanding through partnerships with global manufacturers under the government's *2024–2030 National Automotive Industry Development Strategy*, which targets 500,000 vehicles annually and promotes local [electric vehicle](279350.md) production.[^cite_note-automobiles-212]&#x20;

### Retail and services

See also: [Banking in Egypt](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Banking_in_Egypt)



The [National Bank of Egypt](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/National_Bank_of_Egypt) towers in [Cairo](6293.md)

Egypt's retail and services sector has expanded steadily in recent years, driven by population growth, rising incomes, and urbanisation.[^cite_note-oxford-retail-213] As of 2020 the country's retail market, which covers shopping malls, supermarkets, convenience stores, and e-commerce platforms, was worth around \$200 billion.[^cite_note-oxford-retail-213]&#x20;

The retail food and grocery sector is still largely dominated by traditional, family-run stores and [wet markets](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Wet_markets), although supermarket and convenience store chains expanded in both size and popularity between 2015 and 2020.[^cite_note-oxford-retail-213] [Hypermarkets](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hypermarkets) and [wholesale discounters](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Discount_store) recorded the highest growth rates over this period, at 153% and 162% respectively, accounting for 26% of the sector's sales.[^cite_note-oxford-retail-213]&#x20;

Many shopping malls have opened in Egypt in recent years. [Cairo](6293.md) has the highest concentration with over 20 commercial malls, while smaller cities such as [Mansoura](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mansoura,_Egypt) and [Tanta](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tanta) have only just got their first.[^cite_note-oxford-retail-213]&#x20;

The Egyptian government controls much of the telecommunications sector through its majority ownership of [Telecom Egypt](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Telecom_Egypt).[^cite_note-214] Between 2019 and 2022, mobile internet subscriptions in Egypt grew 77.9%, from 39 million to 69.4 million, with total mobile subscriptions reaching 106.2 million by December 2023.[^cite_note-215] Internet penetration reached 72.2% in early 2024, with 82 million users.[^cite_note-216] Since 2022 Egypt has the fastest internet speeds in Africa.[^cite_note-digitalegy-217]&#x20;

Egyptian banks are among the largest and most dynamic in the region. Most Egyptian lenders are adopting new technologies to expand into nonbanking financial services.[^cite_note-218] The [fintech](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Fintech) sector has grown 5.5-fold since 2020, with 177 startups, ranking Egypt 10th among emerging markets.[^cite_note-fintech-219] The banking sector is dominated by [four state-owned commercial banks](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Big_Four_\(banking\)), the [National Bank of Egypt](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/National_Bank_of_Egypt), [Banque Misr](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Banque_Misr), [Banque du Caire](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Banque_du_Caire), and [Alexbank](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Alexbank), which together account for about 55% of total banking assets.[^cite_note-220] [CIB](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Commercial_International_Bank) is the country's largest private bank and the third-largest overall.[^cite_note-221]&#x20;

### Hydrocarbons and mining

Main articles: [Mining industry of Egypt](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mining_industry_of_Egypt) and [Energy in Egypt](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Energy_in_Egypt)



An offshore platform in the Darfeel Gas Field

Egypt's oil production is concentrated in the Western Desert, the [Gulf of Suez](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Gulf_of_Suez), and the Nile Delta, with peak crude oil output reaching approximately 941,000 bbl/d (149,600 m<sup>3</sup>/d) in 1993 before declining to 630,000 bbl/d (100,000 m<sup>3</sup>/d) by 2008.[^cite_note-The_World_Factbook-222] This decline transformed Egypt into a net oil importer by 2008–2009, prompting a strategic shift towards natural gas.&#x20;

Natural gas has since become central to Egypt's economy, drawing major foreign investment.[^cite_note-The_World_Factbook-222] The [Zohr gas field](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Zohr_gas_field), discovered by [Eni](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Eni) in 2015, is the Mediterranean's largest reserve, containing about 30 trillion cubic feet of gas. Production began in 2017 and reached 2.7 billion cubic feet per day (bcf/d) by 2019,[^cite_note-223] greatly enhancing Egypt's energy outlook, though recent technical issues have reduced output; new drilling is planned to restore capacity.[^cite_note-224] [BP](18998720.md) has made major investments, including a \$12 billion commitment in 2015 to the West Nile Delta project, along with other developments such as Atoll, North Damietta, and Qattameya, which began production in 2020.[^cite_note-225][^cite_note-226]&#x20;

Egypt possesses substantial mineral wealth, with deposits of [gold](12240.md), [copper](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Copper), [iron ore](145352.md), [phosphate](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Phosphate), [uranium](31743.md), [tantalum](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tantalum), [manganese](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Manganese), [chromium](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Chromium), [coal](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Coal), [zinc](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Zinc), [lead](17747.md), [tin](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tin), and black sand minerals such as [ilmenite](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ilmenite), [zircon](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Zircon), [rutile](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Rutile), and [magnetite](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Magnetite).[^cite_note-minerals-227] It also produces abundant industrial materials like [granite](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Granite), [marble](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Marble), [limestone](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Limestone), white sand, [kaolin](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kaolin), and [feldspar](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Feldspar).[^cite_note-minerals-227] The [gold mining](1291393.md) sector has grown rapidly following investment reforms and exploration bids involving [AngloGold Ashanti](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/AngloGold_Ashanti) and [Alexander Nubia International](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Alexander_Nubia_International).[^cite_note-228] The [Sukari mine](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sukari_mine) remains the core operation, with the sector's GDP share expected to reach 5–6% by 2030.[^cite_note-229] Gold exports doubled to \$2.17 billion in 2024, with Egypt aiming to join the world's top ten exporters by 2027.[^cite_note-gold-xin-230]&#x20;

### Agriculture



Farmland in the Egyptian countryside

Egypt's agricultural sector remains essential to its economy, though its share of GDP and employment has declined over time. Egypt produces substantial quantities of [wheat](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Wheat), [maize](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Maize), [sugarcane](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sugarcane), [fruits](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Fruits), [vegetables](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Vegetables), [fodder](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Fodder), and [rice](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Rice), yet remains reliant on wheat and maize imports, primarily from Ukraine and Russia, despite yield improvements since 1970.[^cite_note-IbrahimIbrahim2003-231][^cite_note-232] This dependency stems from high domestic demand, driven by subsidies and a [culinary preference for bread](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Egyptian_cuisine#Bread), as well as Egypt's limited arable land and its emphasis on high-value export crops.[^cite_note-IbrahimIbrahim2003-231] In 2024, Egypt exported \$4.1 billion in agricultural goods[^cite_note-233] and roughly \$6.1 billion in food industry products.[^cite_note-234]&#x20;

The country has achieved self-sufficiency in several key agricultural products, including [vegetables](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Vegetables), [fruits](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Fruits), [poultry](23197.md), [dairy](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Dairy), [eggs](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Eggs_as_food), and [rice](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Rice), while nearing full self-sufficiency in [sugar](27712.md) and [fish](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Fish), with production reaching approximately 90% of demand.[^cite_note-235] Self-sufficiency ratios are lower for [red meat](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Red_meat) (60%),[^cite_note-236] [cereals](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Cereals) (58%), [vegetable oils](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Vegetable_oils) (26%), and [oil crops](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Food_oil) (35%). Consequently, Egypt relies on imports for roughly 45% of its domestic food demand.[^cite_note-237]&#x20;

The [Aswan High Dam](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Aswan_High_Dam), completed in 1971, improved irrigation stability.[^cite_note-238] In 2010, 3.6 million hectares were cultivated, with the government targeting 4.8 million hectares by 2030.[^cite_note-faoun-239] Major initiatives include the New Delta and [Toshka Project](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Toshka_Project), designed to convert desert areas into farmland using advanced irrigation techniques, [wastewater treatment](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Wastewater_treatment), [groundwater](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Groundwater) and water [desalination](156787.md).[^cite_note-240] Efforts to deregulate agriculture have improved price alignment with global markets, but climate change and water scarcity continue to pose long-term challenges.&#x20;

### Tourism

Main article: [Tourism in Egypt](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tourism_in_Egypt)



Tourists riding a [camel](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Arabian_camel) in front of [Pyramid of Khafre](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Pyramid_of_Khafre). The [Giza Necropolis](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Giza_Necropolis) is one of Egypt's main tourist attractions.

Tourism is an important sector in Egypt's economy. Egypt received a record 15.7 million tourists in 2024, up from 14.9 million in 2023. Tourism revenues also rose, to \$14.1 billion.[^cite_note-tourism-241]&#x20;

Egypt's tourism strategy aims to attract 30 million tourists by 2028, with a focus on infrastructure improvements, high-profile projects like the [Grand Egyptian Museum](5359115.md), and enhancing visitor experiences. In 2024, the country's top tourism markets included Germany, Russia, and Saudi Arabia.[^cite_note-tourism-241] The [Giza Necropolis](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Giza_Necropolis) is one of Egypt's best-known tourist attractions; it is the only one of the [Seven Wonders of the Ancient World](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Seven_Wonders_of_the_Ancient_World) still in existence.&#x20;

Egypt's beaches on the Mediterranean and the Red Sea, which extend to over 3,000 kilometres (1,900 miles), are also popular tourist destinations; the [Gulf of Aqaba](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Gulf_of_Aqaba) beaches, [Safaga](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Safaga), [Sharm el-Sheikh](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sharm_el-Sheikh), [Hurghada](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hurghada), [Luxor](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Luxor), [Dahab](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Dahab), [Ras Sidr](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ras_Sidr) and [Marsa Alam](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Marsa_Alam) are popular sites.&#x20;

## Infrastructure

### Energy

Main articles: [Energy in Egypt](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Energy_in_Egypt) and [Electricity sector in Egypt](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Electricity_sector_in_Egypt)



Power plant of the [Aswan High Dam](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Aswan_High_Dam), with the dam itself in the background

Egypt's electricity sector has shifted from full state control to a more diversified energy mix that includes [natural gas](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Fossil_fuel_power_station), [renewables](25784.md), and [nuclear power](22153.md), with growing private sector participation and regional interconnections. [Fossil fuels](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Fossil_fuels) still dominate, generating 88% of electricity in 2023, while hydropower provides 7% and wind and solar together 5%, up from 1% in 2015 but still below the global average of 13% and Africa's 6%.[^cite_note-Egypt-242] Egypt remains Africa's largest producer of gas-fired electricity, accounting for 45% of the continent's total in 2022, yet its per capita carbon emissions remain below the global average.[^cite_note-Egypt-242]&#x20;

The government undertook a series of rapid reforms in response to [blackouts](296636.md) during the summer of 2014 that lasted for up to six hours per day, cutting energy subsidies, and quickly developing the [Zohr gas field](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Zohr_gas_field) in the Mediterranean, which was discovered in 2015. The country now has an oversupply of electricity and aims to source 20% of its electricity from [renewables](25784.md) by 2022 and 55% by 2050.[^cite_note-243] As part of its renewable energy strategy, Egypt has undertaken large-scale projects like the [Benban Solar Park](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Benban_Solar_Park) and the Gabal El Zeit wind farm. Benban, located near Aswan, has a total capacity of 1650 MW and generates about 3.8 TWh annually, making it the fourth-largest solar power plant globally.[^cite_note-One-244] Additionally, the Gabal El Zeit wind farm, costing €340 million, spans 100 square kilometres with 300 turbines, generating 580 MW of electricity.[^cite_note-245][^cite_note-246] Egypt is currently constructing its first nuclear power facility, the [El Dabaa Nuclear Power Plant](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/El_Dabaa_Nuclear_Power_Plant), in the northern coastal region.[^cite_note-247]&#x20;

Egypt and [Cyprus](5593.md) are considering implementing the proposed [EuroAfrica Interconnector](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/EuroAfrica_Interconnector) project.[^cite_note-EA-248] This consists of laying a 2 GW [HVDC](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/High-voltage_direct_current) [undersea power cable](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Submarine_power_cable) between them and between Cyprus and Greece, thus connecting Egypt to the greater European power grid.[^cite_note-249] The interconnector will make Egypt an electricity hub between Europe and Africa.[^cite_note-250] The president of Egypt and the prime minister of Cyprus met in [Nicosia](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Nicosia) on 21 November 2017 and showed their full support for the EuroAfrica Interconnector pointing out its importance for their energy security.[^cite_note-251][^cite_note-252]&#x20;

### Transport

Main article: [Transport in Egypt](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Transport_in_Egypt)



The [Cairo Metro](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Cairo_Metro)

Transport in Egypt is primarily concentrated around Cairo, following the Nile River's settlement patterns. The country's 4,800-kilometre (3,000 mi) railway network is operated by [Egyptian National Railways](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Egyptian_National_Railways), with its main line running from [Alexandria](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Alexandria) to [Aswan](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Aswan). In 2021, Egypt launched a \$4.5 billion [high-speed rail project](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/High-speed_rail_in_Egypt) to connect [Ain Sokhna](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ain_Sokhna), [Mersa Matruh](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mersa_Matruh), and Alexandria, later expanding to include routes linking [Greater Cairo](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Greater_Cairo) to Aswan and [Luxor](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Luxor) to [Hurghada](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hurghada) and [Safaga](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Safaga).[^cite_note-253][^cite_note-2022-05-30-rt-254]&#x20;

The [Cairo Metro](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Cairo_Metro) operates three lines, with a fourth line under construction and plans for additional expansions in the future. Meanwhile, two [monorail lines](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Cairo_Monorail) are also being built in the city, along with a [metro system](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Alexandria_Metro) in [Alexandria](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Alexandria).[^cite_note-255]&#x20;

Egypt has undertaken major road infrastructure expansion through the [National Roads Project](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/National_Roads_Project), increasing the total length of main roads by nearly 30% from 23,500 km in 2014 to 30,500 km in 2024. Alongside constructing new highways, thousands of kilometres of roads have been upgraded. Egypt's ranking in the Road Quality Index rose from 118th place in 2015 to 18th place in 2024.[^cite_note-256]&#x20;

Egypt's aviation sector is led by [EgyptAir](319357.md), the country's [flag carrier](465816.md) and largest airline, founded in 1932 by [Talaat Harb](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Talaat_Harb). Now state-owned, EgyptAir operates from its [Cairo International Airport](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Cairo_International_Airport) hub, with scheduled passenger and cargo services to over 75 destinations across the Middle East, Europe, Africa, Asia, and the Americas. The airline's current fleet consists of 80 aircraft.&#x20;

The [Suez Canal](29323.md), an artificial sea-level waterway, connects the Mediterranean Sea and the Red Sea, enabling direct shipping between Europe and Asia without navigating around Africa. In 2023, it generated a record \$9.4 billion in revenue for Egypt.[^cite_note-257] Opened in November 1869, the 193.30 km (120+1⁄8 mi) canal runs from [Port Said](205162.md) in the north to [Port Tawfiq](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Port_Tawfiq) in the south, with [Ismailia](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ismailia) situated 3 kilometres (1+7⁄8 miles) west of its midpoint.[^cite_note-SuezExpansion-258]&#x20;



The [Suez Canal Bridge](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Suez_Canal_Bridge)

The canal is 24 metres (79 feet) deep and 205 m (673 ft) wide as of 2010, consisting of a 22 km (14 mi) northern access channel, a 162.25 km (100+7⁄8 mi) main section, and a 9 km (5+1⁄2 mi) southern access channel. The Ballah By-Pass and the Great Bitter Lake provide passing points along the canal, which operates without locks, allowing seawater to flow freely. [Expansions](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/New_Suez_Canal) completed in 2015 increased its daily capacity from 49 to 97 ships, reducing transit times and boosting global maritime trade.[^cite_note-SuezExpansion-258][^cite_note-259][^cite_note-260]&#x20;

### Water supply and sanitation

Main article: [Water supply and sanitation in Egypt](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Water_supply_and_sanitation_in_Egypt)



Green irrigated land along the Nile amidst the desert and in the [Nile Delta](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Nile_Delta)

Egypt's [water supply](684928.md) is heavily dependent on the [Nile River](21244.md), which provides approximately 90% of the country's total water resources, with an annual share of 55 billion cubic metres, unchanged since 1954. An additional 0.5 billion cubic metres comes from non-renewable groundwater sources. However, national demand exceeds 90 billion cubic metres annually, creating a persistent water deficit. As a result, Egypt's per capita water share fell to 570 cubic metres in 2018, well below the 1,000 cubic metre international water scarcity threshold. To address this, the government has prioritised efficient water management, particularly in response to population growth and agricultural expansion.[^cite_note-sanitation-261]&#x20;

Even so, water access has improved sharply. Between 1990 and 2010, piped water coverage rose from 89% to 100% in urban areas and from 39% to 93% in rural areas, eliminating [open defecation](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Open_defecation) in rural regions and achieving near-universal access to an [improved water source](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Improved_water_source). By 2015, 90% of the population had access to safely managed drinking water, increasing to 96.9% by 2019. Similarly, proper sanitation coverage expanded from 50% in 2015 to 66.2% in 2019, while the percentage of treated wastewater relative to total wastewater rose from 50% to 68.7% in the same period, reaching 74% in 2022.[^cite_note-262] However, gaps in sanitation infrastructure have historically led to health concerns, with a 2007 report estimating 17,000 child deaths annually from diarrhoeal diseases linked to poor sanitation.[^cite_note-IDRC-263]&#x20;

Egypt has made significant investments in wastewater treatment infrastructure as part of its broader water management strategy. In 2021, the Bahr El Baqar Wastewater Treatment Plant was completed, becoming the largest in the world at the time, with a capacity of 5 million cubic metres per day. The treated water from this facility is allocated to irrigating 342,000 acres under the Sinai Peninsula Development Plan.[^cite_note-sanitation-261] In 2023, Egypt inaugurated the [New Delta Wastewater Treatment Plant](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/New_Delta_Wastewater_Treatment_Plant), surpassing Bahr El Baqar as the largest wastewater facility globally, with a capacity of 7.5 million cubic metres per day.[^cite_note-treatmentplant-264] A key component of Egypt's agricultural expansion strategy, the plant supports the New Delta reclamation project while also reducing pollution in [Lake Mariout](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Lake_Mariout) and the [Mediterranean Sea](19006.md).[^cite_note-treatmentplant-264]&#x20;

Given Egypt's arid climate and lack of appreciable rainfall, agriculture is entirely dependent on irrigation. The Nile River, regulated by the [Aswan High Dam](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Aswan_High_Dam), releases an annual average of 55 billion cubic metres, of which 46 billion cubic metres are diverted into irrigation canals.[^cite_note-ewup-265] This irrigation sustains 33,600 square kilometres (13,000 square miles) of agricultural land in the Nile Valley and Nile Delta, producing an average of 1.8 crops per year.[^cite_note-ewup-265]&#x20;

## Demographics

Main articles: [Demographics of Egypt](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Demographics_of_Egypt) and [Egyptians](31912046.md)



Egypt's population density in 2010 (people per km

2

)

Egypt is the most populated country in the Arab world and the third most populous on the [African continent](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/African_continent), with about 120 million inhabitants as of 2026.[^cite_note-mospop-16] Its population grew rapidly from 1970 to 2010 due to [medical advances](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/History_of_medicine#Late_modern_medicine) and increases in agricultural productivity[^cite_note-266] enabled by the [Green Revolution](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Green_Revolution).[^cite_note-267] Egypt's population was estimated at 3 million when [Napoleon](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Napoleon_I_of_France) invaded the country in 1798.[^cite_note-268] The great majority of its people live near the banks of the [Nile](21244.md) River, an area of about 40,000 square kilometres (15,000 sq mi), where the only [arable land](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Arable_land) is found. The large regions of the [Sahara](325363.md) desert, which constitute most of Egypt's territory, are sparsely inhabited. About 43% of Egypt's residents live across the country's urban areas,[^cite_note-269] with most spread across the densely populated centres of greater Cairo, Alexandria and other major cities in the Nile Delta.&#x20;

Egypt's people are highly urbanised, being concentrated along the Nile (notably Cairo and Alexandria), in the Delta and near the Suez Canal. Egyptians are divided demographically into those who live in the major urban centres and the [fellahin](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Fellah), or farmers, that reside in rural villages. The total inhabited area constitutes only 77,041 km<sup>2</sup>, putting the [physiological density](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Physiological_density) at over 1,200 people per km<sup>2</sup>, similar to Bangladesh.&#x20;

While emigration was restricted under Nasser, thousands of Egyptian professionals were dispatched abroad in the context of the [Arab Cold War](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Arab_Cold_War).[^cite_note-270] Egyptian emigration was liberalised in 1971, under President Sadat, reaching record numbers after the 1973 oil crisis.[^cite_note-271] An estimated 2.7 million Egyptians live abroad. Approximately 70% of Egyptian migrants live in Arab countries (923,600 in Saudi Arabia, 332,600 in Libya, 226,850 in Jordan, 190,550 in Kuwait with the rest elsewhere in the region) and the remaining 30% reside mostly in Europe and North America (318,000 in the United States, 110,000 in Canada and 90,000 in Italy).[^cite_note-IOMEgypt-272] The process of emigrating to non-Arab states has been ongoing since the 1950s.[^cite_note-273]&#x20;

### Ethnic groups

Ethnic [Egyptians](31912046.md) are by far the largest ethnic group in the country, constituting 99.7% of the total population.[^cite_note-factbook-his-82] Ethnic minorities include the [Circassians](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Circassians), [Turks](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Turkish_people), [Greeks](42056.md), [Bedouin](61770.md) Arab tribes living in the eastern deserts and the [Sinai Peninsula](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sinai_Peninsula), the [Berber](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Berber_language)-speaking [Siwis](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Siwis) ([Amazigh](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Berber_people)) of the [Siwa Oasis](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Siwa_Oasis), and the [Nubian](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Nubian_people) communities clustered along the Nile. There are also tribal [Beja](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Beja_people) communities concentrated in the southeasternmost corner of the country, and a number of [Dom](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Dom_people) clans mostly in the Nile Delta and [Faiyum](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Faiyum) who are progressively becoming assimilated as urbanisation increases.&#x20;

Egypt hosts a migrant population of over 9 million, constituting 8.7% of the country's total population, according to the International Organization for Migration. These migrants originate from 133 countries, with the largest groups being [Sudanese](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sudanese) (4 million), [Syrians](8862873.md) (1.5 million), [Yemenis](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Yemenis) (1 million), and [Libyans](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Libyans) (1 million), collectively making up 80% of all international migrants in Egypt.[^cite_note-274]&#x20;

### Languages

Main article: [Languages of Egypt](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Languages_of_Egypt)

The [official language](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Official_language) of Egypt is [Literary Arabic](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Modern_Standard_Arabic).[^cite_note-275] The [spoken languages](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Spoken_language) are: [Egyptian Arabic](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Egyptian_Arabic) (68%), [Sa'idi Arabic](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sa%27idi_Arabic) (29%), [Eastern Egyptian Bedawi Arabic](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bedawi_Arabic) (1.6%), [Sudanese Arabic](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sudanese_Arabic) (0.6%), [Domari](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Domari_language) (0.3%), [Nobiin](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Nobiin_language) (0.3%),[^cite_note-276] [Beja](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Beja_language) (0.1%), [Siwi](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Siwi_language) and others. Additionally, Greek, [Armenian](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Armenian_language) and Italian, and more recently, African languages like [Amharic](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Amharic_language) and [Tigrigna](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tigrinya_language) are the main languages of immigrants.&#x20;

The main foreign languages taught in schools, by order of popularity, are English, French, German and Italian.&#x20;

Historically [Egyptian](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Egyptian_language) was spoken, the latest stage of which is [Coptic Egyptian](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Coptic_language). Spoken Coptic was mostly extinct by the 17th century but may have survived in isolated pockets in [Upper Egypt](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Upper_Egypt) as late as the 19th century. It remains in use as the liturgical language of the [Coptic Orthodox Church of Alexandria](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Coptic_Orthodox_Church_of_Alexandria).[^cite_note-extinct-277][^cite_note-Daily_Star_Egypt-278] It forms a separate branch among the family of [Afroasiatic languages](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Afroasiatic_languages).&#x20;

### Religion

Main article: [Religion in Egypt](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Religion_in_Egypt)



[Madrasa-Mosque of Sultan Hassan](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mosque-Madrassa_of_Sultan_Hassan)

Islam is the [state religion](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/State_religion) of Egypt. Egypt has the largest Muslim population in the [Arab world](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Arab_world) and the world's [sixth largest Muslim population](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Islam_by_country), accounting for five percent of all Muslims worldwide.[^cite_note-PewDec2012-279] Egypt also has the [largest Christian population](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Christianity_in_the_Middle_East) in the [Middle East and North Africa](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Middle_East_and_North_Africa).[^cite_note-Global_Christianity-280] Official data about religion is lacking due to social and political sensitivities.[^cite_note-281] An estimated 85–90% are identified as Muslim, 10–15% as [Coptic Christians](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Coptic_Christians), and 1% as other Christian denominations; other estimates place the Christian population as high as 15–20%.[^cite_note-287]&#x20;

Egypt was an early and leading centre of Christianity into [late antiquity](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Late_antiquity). The [Coptic Orthodox Church of Alexandria](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Coptic_Orthodox_Church_of_Alexandria) was founded in the first century and remains the largest church in Egypt. With the arrival of Islam in the seventh century, Egypt was gradually Islamised into a majority-Muslim country.[^cite_note-288][^cite_note-289] It is unknown when Muslims reached a majority, variously estimated from c. 1000 CE to as late as the 14th century. Egypt emerged as a centre of politics and culture in the [Muslim world](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Muslim_world). Under [Anwar Sadat](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Anwar_Sadat), Islam became the official [state religion](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/State_religion) and [Sharia](28840.md) the main source of law.[^cite_note-georgetown-67]&#x20;



[St. Mark Coptic Cathedral in Alexandria](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Saint_Mark%27s_Coptic_Orthodox_Cathedral_\(Alexandria\))

The majority of Egyptian Muslims adhere to the [Sunni branch of Islam](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sunni_Islam). [Nondenominational Muslims](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Non-denominational_Muslims) form roughly 12% of the population.[^cite_note-churches-deleg-5][^cite_note-290] There is also a [Shi'a](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Shi%27a) minority. The [Jerusalem Centre for Public Affairs](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jerusalem_Center_for_Public_Affairs) estimates the Shia population at 1 to 2.2 million[^cite_note-291] and could measure as much as 3 million.[^cite_note-292] The [Ahmadiyya](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ahmadiyya) population is estimated at less than 50,000,[^cite_note-293] whereas the [Salafi](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Salafi) (ultra-conservative Sunni) population is estimated at five to six million.[^cite_note-294]&#x20;

[Cairo](6293.md) is famous for its numerous mosque [minarets](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Minaret) and has been dubbed "The City of 1,000 Minarets".[^cite_note-295] The city also hosts [Al-Azhar University](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Al-Azhar_University), which is considered the preeminent institution of Islamic higher learning and jurisprudence.[^cite_note-296] Founded in the late tenth century, it is by some measures the second oldest continuously operating university in the world.[^cite_note-297]&#x20;

It is estimated that 15 million Egyptians follow native Sufi [orders](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tariqah),[^cite_note-298][^cite_note-HuffPost15Million-299][^cite_note-300] with [Sufi](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sufism) leadership asserting that the numbers are much greater, as many Egyptian Sufis are not officially registered with a Sufi order.[^cite_note-HuffPost15Million-299] At least 305 people were killed during a [November 2017 attack](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2017_Sinai_mosque_attack) on a Sufi mosque in Sinai.[^cite_note-301]&#x20;

Of the [Christian population in Egypt](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Christianity_in_Egypt), over 90% belong to the native Coptic Orthodox Church of Alexandria, an [Oriental Orthodox](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Oriental_Orthodox) Christian Church.[^cite_note-302] Other native Egyptian Christians are adherents of the [Coptic Catholic Church](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Coptic_Catholic_Church), the [Evangelical Church of Egypt](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Evangelical_Church_of_Egypt_\(Synod_of_the_Nile\)) and various other [Protestant](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Protestant) denominations. Non-native Christian communities are largely found in the urban regions of Cairo and Alexandria, such as the [Syro-Lebanese](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Syro-Lebanese_in_Egypt), who belong to [Greek Catholic](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Melkite_Greek_Catholic_Church), [Greek Orthodox](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Greek_Orthodox), and [Maronite Catholic](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Maronite_Catholic) denominations.[^cite_note-303]&#x20;

The Egyptian government recognises only three religions: Islam, Christianity, and Judaism. Other faiths and minority Muslim sects, such as the small [Baháʼí Faith](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bah%C3%A1%CA%BC%C3%AD_Faith) and [Ahmadiyya](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ahmadiyya) communities, are not recognised by the state and face persecution by the government, which labels these groups a threat to Egypt's national security.[^cite_note-304][^cite_note-305] Individuals, particularly Baháʼís and atheists, wishing to include their religion (or lack thereof) on their mandatory state issued identification cards [are denied this ability](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Egyptian_identification_card_controversy), and were put in the position of either not obtaining required identification or lying about their faith. A 2008 court ruling allowed members of unrecognised faiths to obtain identification and leave the religion field blank.[^cite_note-International_Religious_Freedom_Report_2008-306][^cite_note-reuters-307]&#x20;

### Education

Main article: [Education in Egypt](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Education_in_Egypt)



Egyptian literacy rate among the population aged 15 years and older by UNESCO Institute of Statistics

In 2022, Egypt's adult literacy rate was 74.5%, compared to 71.1% in 2017.[^cite_note-308] Literacy is lowest among those over 65 years of age, at 32.9% in 2021,[^cite_note-309] and highest among youth between 15 and 24 years of age, at 92.2% in 2022.[^cite_note-310]&#x20;



[Cairo University](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Cairo_University)

A European-style education system was first introduced in Egypt by the Ottomans in the early 19th century to nurture a class of loyal bureaucrats and army officers.[^cite_note-edu-chatham-311] Under British occupation, investment in education was curbed drastically, and secular public schools, which had previously been free, began to charge fees.[^cite_note-edu-chatham-311]&#x20;

In the 1950s, President Nasser phased in free education for all Egyptians.[^cite_note-edu-chatham-311] The Egyptian curriculum influenced other Arab education systems, which often employed Egyptian-trained teachers.[^cite_note-edu-chatham-311] Demand soon outstripped the level of available state resources, causing the quality of public education to deteriorate.[^cite_note-edu-chatham-311] Today this trend has culminated in poor teacher–student ratios (often around one to fifty) and persistent gender inequality.[^cite_note-edu-chatham-311]&#x20;

Basic education, which includes six years of primary and three years of preparatory school, is a right for Egyptian children from the age of six.[^cite_note-oecd-edu-312] After grade 9, students are tracked into one of two strands of secondary education: general or technical schools. General secondary education prepares students for further education, and graduates of this track normally join higher education institutes based on the results of the [Thanaweya Amma](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Thanaweya_Amma), the leaving exam.[^cite_note-oecd-edu-312] In 2025 Egypt introduced a new Baccalaureate Certificate System which will replace the Thanaweya Amma starting 2026.[^cite_note-313]&#x20;

Technical secondary education has two strands, one lasting three years and a more advanced education lasting five. Graduates of these schools may have access to higher education based on their results on the final exam, but this is generally uncommon.[^cite_note-oecd-edu-312]&#x20;

The [QS World University Rankings](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/QS_World_University_Rankings) 2025 includes 15 Egyptian universities, with [Cairo University](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Cairo_University) ranked highest among them. The [American University in Cairo](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/American_University_in_Cairo) follows, improving its position to 410th place. Several Egyptian universities rose in the rankings compared with the previous year.[^cite_note-314]&#x20;

Egypt is opening new research institutes to modernise scientific research and development. The most recent is [Zewail City of Science and Technology](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Zewail_City_of_Science_and_Technology). Egypt was ranked 86th in the [Global Innovation Index](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Global_Innovation_Index) in 2025.[^cite_note-315][^cite_note-316]&#x20;

### Health

Main articles: [Health in Egypt](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Health_in_Egypt) and [Healthcare in Egypt](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Healthcare_in_Egypt)



[Egypt's children cancer hospital](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Children%27s_Cancer_Hospital_Egypt) known as *57357 hospital*

As of 2024, Egypt's average life expectancy is 75 years, with 73.8 years for males and 76.2 years for females.[^cite_note-cia-health-317] Healthcare access has improved in both urban and rural areas, with immunisation programmes now covering 98% of the population. Life expectancy has risen from 45 years in the 1960s, as medical services and disease prevention improved. The infant mortality rate has also declined sharply, dropping from 101 to 132 deaths per 1,000 live births in the 1970s and 1980s to 50–60 per 1,000 in 2000, and further to 16–18 per 1,000 in 2024.[^cite_note-318][^cite_note-cia-health-317]&#x20;

The [Ministry of Health and Population](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ministry_of_Health_and_Population_\(Egypt\)) oversees most public hospitals, while the [Ministry of Higher Education and Scientific Research](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ministry_of_Higher_Education_and_Scientific_Research_\(Egypt\)) manages university hospitals, offering free medical services. Other ministries operate hospitals for employees, and the [Ministry of Defence](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ministry_of_Defence_\(Egypt\)) and [Ministry of Interior](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ministry_of_Interior_\(Egypt\)) run facilities that require out-of-pocket payments for non-service members.[^cite_note-healthcare1-319]&#x20;

The private sector provides about 60% of healthcare services, including for-profit and nonprofit organisations, private hospitals, pharmacies, and independent practitioners. Numerous [nongovernmental organisations](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Nongovernmental_organizations) also offer healthcare, including religious and charitable institutions. As of 2021, Egypt had 1,145 private hospitals, a 23.69% increase since 2011.[^cite_note-320] Private healthcare facilities in Egypt are generally of high quality.[^cite_note-321]&#x20;

Egypt grants refugees and asylum-seekers access to public healthcare on equal terms with citizens, allowing them to receive care at primary health facilities either free or at low cost.[^cite_note-322]&#x20;

A 2008 report by the [World Health Organisation](33583.md) estimated that 91.1% of Egypt's girls and women aged 15 to 49 have been subjected to [genital mutilation](11408.md). Government measures have reduced the practice among younger generations. By 2014, rates had dropped to 10.4% in urban areas and 15.9% in rural areas among girls aged 1–14. In June 2025 the [Minister of Social Solidarity](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ministry_of_Social_Solidarity) announced that the percentage of girls aged 15 to 17 who had undergone the practice dropped to 37 percent in 2021, compared to 61 percent in 2014. Public support for FGM has also fallen, with the proportion of women endorsing the practice decreasing from 75 percent in 2000 to 30 percent in 2021. Legislative reforms, awareness campaigns, and stricter law enforcement contributed to this decline. In 2016, penalties for performing the practice were increased, with prison sentences of up to 15 years for practitioners and up to 3 years for [guardians](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Legal_guardian#Guardianship_for_minors) who facilitate the procedure.[^cite_note-323][^cite_note-324][^cite_note-325]&#x20;
